Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
2. Directory structure is hierarchical (tree structure) with root "/"
3. Paths can be absolute (start with "/") or relative (start with directory name or "..")
4. ".." means go to parent directory
5. We need to track the current directory path and when we get a `pwd` command, output the absolute path
6. The output path should not contain ".." and must end with a slash

## Key Steps

1. Maintain a list representing the current directory path (from root to current)
2. For `cd` command:
   - If path starts with "/", it's absolute - reset the path and parse the rest
   - Otherwise, it's relative - parse the path from current position
   - Process each part: directory name or ".."
   - ".." means remove the last directory from path
   - Directory name means add it to path
3. For `pwd` command:
   - Join all elements in current path with "/" and add trailing slash
   - Print the result

## Detailed Algorithm

1. Initialize current path as [""] (or just empty list, since root is represented as empty)
2. For each command:
   - If it's "pwd": output the current path formatted
   - If it's "cd": parse the path parameter and update current directory

## Edge Cases

- Root path: "/root" or "/home" should go to root
- Multiple ".." in path
- Path with just "..": should go to root (but problem says no attempt to go above root)
- Empty directory names? No, problem states non-empty

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names, empty means root

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip().split()
    
    if command[0] == "pwd":
        # Format the path: join with "/" and add trailing slash
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            path_str = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(path_str)
    
    elif command[0] == "cd":
        path_str = command[1]
        
        # If path starts with "/", it's absolute - start from root
        if path_str.startswith('/'):
            # Remove the leading slash and split by '/'
            path_parts = path_str[1:].split('/')
            current_path = []
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only pop if not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # Skip empty parts (though the problem guarantees no consecutive slashes)
                    current_path.append(part)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            path_parts = path_str.split('/')
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only pop if not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # Skip empty parts
                    current_path.append(part)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about empty parts. Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1:
- Start: current_path = []
- pwd → "/" 
- cd /home/vasya → path_parts = ["home", "vasya"] → current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → "/home/vasya/"
- cd .. → path_parts = [".."] → current_path = ["home"]
- pwd → "/home/"
- cd vasya/../petya → path_parts = ["vasya", "..", "petya"] 
  - "vasya" → add → ["home", "vasya"]
  - ".." → pop → ["home"]
  - "petya" → add → ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya/"

This looks correct.

Let me check the second example:
- cd /a/b → path_parts = ["a", "b"] → current_path = ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"
- cd ../a/b → path_parts = ["..", "a", "b"]
  - ".." → pop → ["a", "b"] → becomes []
  - "a" → add → ["a"]
  - "b" → add → ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"

Wait, this gives "/a/b/" but expected is "/a/a/b/" - that doesn't match.

Let me re-read the example input:
Input:
4
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd

Expected output:
/a/b/
/a/a/b/

Ah, I see. When we do cd ../a/b from current path /a/b:
- The path is "../a/b"
- First part: ".." → goes to parent of current directory → from /a/b to /a
- Then "a" → goes to /a/a
- Then "b" → goes to /a/a/b

So current_path should be:
- Initially: ["a", "b"] after cd /a/b
- After cd ../a/b: 
  - ".." → pop → ["a"]
  - "a" → append → ["a", "a"] 
  - "b" → append → ["a", "a", "b"]

Wait, that would be /a/a/b, which matches expected.

But when we parse "../a/b" from current directory /a/b, we go:
- Start from /a/b
- ".." → go to parent: /a
- Then "a" → /a/a
- Then "b" → /a/a/b

So current_path should be ["a", "a", "b"]

In my algorithm, after ".." from ["a", "b"]:
- current_path becomes ["a"] 
- Then "a" → becomes ["a", "a"]
- Then "b" → becomes ["a", "a", "b"]

This gives "/a/a/b/", which is correct.

But the expected output shows "/a/a/b/", which is correct.

Wait, the example says:
Input:
4
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd

Output:
/a/b/
/a/a/b/

So my algorithm should work.

One more thing: what if the path is just "/"? 
- path_str = "/" 
- It starts with "/", so we remove leading slash → path_parts = []
- Then current_path = [] → which is correct for root

What if path is "/.."?
- Starts with "/", path_parts = [".."] 
- ".." → current_path.pop() → but current_path is empty → should be safe

The problem states: "It is guaranteed that there is no attempt of transition to the parent directory of the root directory."

So we don't need to handle empty pop.

Final code:

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names, empty means root

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip().split()
    
    if command[0] == "pwd":
        # Format the path: join with "/" and add trailing slash
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            path_str = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(path_str)
    
    elif command[0] == "cd":
        path_str = command[1]
        
        # If path starts with "/", it's absolute - start from root
        if path_str.startswith('/'):
            # Remove the leading slash and split by '/'
            parts = path_str[1:].split('/')
            current_path = []
            for part in parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only pop if not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # Skip empty parts
                    current_path.append(part)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            parts = path_str.split('/')
            for part in parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only pop if not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # Skip empty parts
                    current_path.append(part)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Vasya is writing an operating system shell, and it should have commands for working with directories. To begin with, he decided to go with just two commands: cd (change the current directory) and pwd (display the current directory).

Directories in Vasya's operating system form a traditional hierarchical tree structure. There is a single root directory, denoted by the slash character "/". Every other directory has a name — a non-empty string consisting of lowercase Latin letters. Each directory (except for the root) has a parent directory — the one that contains the given directory. It is denoted as "..".

The command cd takes a single parameter, which is a path in the file system. The command changes the current directory to the directory specified by the path. The path consists of the names of directories separated by slashes. The name of the directory can be "..", which means a step up to the parent directory. «..» can be used in any place of the path, maybe several times. If the path begins with a slash, it is considered to be an absolute path, that is, the directory changes to the specified one, starting from the root. If the parameter begins with a directory name (or ".."), it is considered to be a relative path, that is, the directory changes to the specified directory, starting from the current one.

The command pwd should display the absolute path to the current directory. This path must not contain "..".

Initially, the current directory is the root. All directories mentioned explicitly or passed indirectly within any command cd are considered to exist. It is guaranteed that there is no attempt of transition to the parent directory of the root directory.

Input

The first line of the input data contains the single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 50) — the number of commands.

Then follow n lines, each contains one command. Each of these lines contains either command pwd, or command cd, followed by a space-separated non-empty parameter.

The command parameter cd only contains lower case Latin letters, slashes and dots, two slashes cannot go consecutively, dots occur only as the name of a parent pseudo-directory. The command parameter cd does not end with a slash, except when it is the only symbol that points to the root directory. The command parameter has a length from 1 to 200 characters, inclusive.

Directories in the file system can have the same names.

Output

For each command pwd you should print the full absolute path of the given directory, ending with a slash. It should start with a slash and contain the list of slash-separated directories in the order of being nested from the root to the current folder. It should contain no dots.

Examples

Input

7
pwd
cd /home/vasya
pwd
cd ..
pwd
cd vasya/../petya
pwd


Output

/
/home/vasya/
/home/
/home/petya/


Input

4
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd


Output

/a/b/
/a/a/b/### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
2. The file system is a hierarchical tree with root `/`
3. Directories can have names with lowercase letters
4. `..` means go to parent directory
5. Paths can be absolute (start with `/`) or relative (start with directory name)
6. `pwd` should output the absolute path ending with `/`
7. We start at the root directory

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Track the current directory
- We need to maintain a list of directories (path components) from root to current
- Initially, this list is empty (root)

### Step 2: Handle cd command
- If the path starts with `/`, it's absolute - we reset to root and process the rest
- Otherwise, it's relative - we process from current position
- Split the path by `/` to get components
- For each component:
  - If it's "..", remove the last component (go to parent)
  - If it's a directory name, add it to the path
- Special case: if the path is just "/", it means root

### Step 3: Handle pwd command
- Convert the current path list into a string with slashes
- Ensure it ends with `/`

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Use a list to represent the current path components
- When processing a path component:
  - Skip empty strings (from consecutive slashes)
  - For "..", pop from the list if not empty
  - For other names, append to the list

Let me trace through the first example:
1. Start: path = []
2. pwd → `/`
3. cd /home/vasya → path becomes ["home", "vasya"]
4. pwd → `/home/vasya/`
5. cd .. → path becomes ["home"]
6. pwd → `/home/`
7. cd vasya/../petya → 
   - Relative path: vasya/../petya
   - "vasya" → add to path → ["home", "vasya"]
   - ".." → remove last → ["home"]
   - "petya" → add → ["home", "petya"]
8. pwd → `/home/petya/`

Wait, let me recheck the example. Looking at the input "cd vasya/../petya":
- This should be relative from current directory which is /home
- So: from /home, go to vasya → /home/vasya, then go to parent → /home, then go to petya → /home/petya

The path components should be processed as:
- "vasya" → add to current path → ["home", "vasya"]
- ".." → remove last → ["home"] 
- "petya" → add → ["home", "petya"]

Yes, that matches.

## Code Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names from root to current

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith('pwd'):
        # Convert current path to string with slashes
        path_str = '/' + '/'.join(current_path) + '/'
        print(path_str)
    
    elif command.startswith('cd'):
        # Extract the path parameter
        path_param = command.split(' ')[1]
        
        # If it's an absolute path (starts with /), reset current_path and process from root
        if path_param.startswith('/'):
            current_path = []
            # Remove the leading slash and split
            path_components = path_param[1:].split('/')
        else:
            # Relative path - process from current directory
            path_components = path_param.split('/')
        
        # Process each component
        for component in path_components:
            if component == '..':
                if current_path:  # if not at root
                    current_path.pop()
            elif component:  # non-empty component
                current_path.append(component)
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
2. Directory structure is hierarchical (tree-based) with root "/"
3. Paths can be absolute (start with "/") or relative (start with directory name or "..")
4. ".." means go to parent directory
5. We need to track the current directory path and update it with cd commands
6. pwd outputs the absolute path ending with "/"

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Analyze the flow
- Start with current directory = root ("/")
- For each command:
  - If "pwd": print current path
  - If "cd path": update current directory based on path

### Step 2: Handle path parsing
- If path starts with "/", it's absolute - start from root
- Otherwise, it's relative - start from current directory
- Split path by "/" to get components
- Process each component:
  - If component is "..": go up one level (remove last directory)
  - If component is a valid directory name: go down to that directory
- Special case: empty component after splitting (due to trailing slash) should be ignored

### Step 3: Implement path processing
- We'll maintain a list of directory names in the current path
- For absolute path: clear current path and add components
- For relative path: process components starting from current path

### Step 4: Edge cases
- Path like "/home/vasya" - absolute, starts with "/"
- Path like "home/vasya" - relative, starts with letter
- Path like ".." - go to parent
- Path like "/.." - go to root (since ".." from root doesn't exist, but problem guarantees no attempt to go parent of root)

Let me trace through the first example:
1. pwd → "/" 
2. cd /home/vasya → becomes "/home/vasya"
3. pwd → "/home/vasya"
4. cd .. → goes up to "/home" 
5. pwd → "/home"
6. cd vasya/../petya → start from current "/home"
   - vasya: go to "/home/vasya"
   - ..: go to "/home"
   - petya: go to "/home/petya"
7. pwd → "/home/petya"

### Step 5: Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names in current path

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("cd"):
        path_part = command[3:].strip()
        
        if path_part.startswith('/'):
            # Absolute path - start from root
            current_path = []
            components = path_part[1:].split('/')  # remove leading '/'
            for comp in components:
                if comp == '':  # empty component
                    continue
                if comp == '..':
                    if current_path:  # only go up if we have directories
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(comp)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            components = path_part.split('/')
            for comp in components:
                if comp == '':
                    continue
                if comp == '..':
                    if current_path:
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(comp)
                    
    elif command == "pwd":
        # Build the absolute path with slashes
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            result = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(result)
```

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names in current path

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("cd"):
        path_part = command[3:].strip()
        
        if path_part.startswith('/'):
            # Absolute path - start from root
            current_path = []
            components = path_part[1:].split('/')  # remove leading '/'
            for comp in components:
                if comp == '':  # empty component
                    continue
                if comp == '..':
                    if current_path:  # only go up if we have directories
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(comp)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            components = path_part.split('/')
            for comp in components:
                if comp == '':
                    continue
                if comp == '..':
                    if current_path:
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(comp)
                    
    elif command == "pwd":
        # Build the absolute path with slashes
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            result = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(result)
```

Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Vasya is writing an operating system shell, and it should have commands for working with directories. To begin with, he decided to go with just two commands: cd (change the current directory) and pwd (display the current directory).

Directories in Vasya's operating system form a traditional hierarchical tree structure. There is a single root directory, denoted by the slash character "/". Every other directory has a name — a non-empty string consisting of lowercase Latin letters. Each directory (except for the root) has a parent directory — the one that contains the given directory. It is denoted as "..".

The command cd takes a single parameter, which is a path in the file system. The command changes the current directory to the directory specified by the path. The path consists of the names of directories separated by slashes. The name of the directory can be "..", which means a step up to the parent directory. «..» can be used in any place of the path, maybe several times. If the path begins with a slash, it is considered to be an absolute path, that is, the directory changes to the specified one, starting from the root. If the parameter begins with a directory name (or ".."), it is considered to be a relative path, that is, the directory changes to the specified directory, starting from the current one.

The command pwd should display the absolute path to the current directory. This path must not contain "..".

Initially, the current directory is the root. All directories mentioned explicitly or passed indirectly within any command cd are considered to exist. It is guaranteed that there is no attempt of transition to the parent directory of the root directory.

Input

The first line of the input data contains the single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 50) — the number of commands.

Then follow n lines, each contains one command. Each of these lines contains either command pwd, or command cd, followed by a space-separated non-empty parameter.

The command parameter cd only contains lower case Latin letters, slashes and dots, two slashes cannot go consecutively, dots occur only as the name of a parent pseudo-directory. The command parameter cd does not end with a slash, except when it is the only symbol that points to the root directory. The command parameter has a length from 1 to 200 characters, inclusive.

Directories in the file system can have the same names.

Output

For each command pwd you should print the full absolute path of the given directory, ending with a slash. It should start with a slash and contain the list of slash-separated directories in the order of being nested from the root to the current folder. It should contain no dots.

Examples

Input

7
pwd
cd /home/vasya
pwd
cd ..
pwd
cd vasya/../petya
pwd


Output

/
/home/vasya/
/home/
/home/petya/


Input

4
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd


Output

/a/b/
/a/a/b/### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a file system with a hierarchical tree structure starting from root "/"
2. Two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
3. `cd` path can be absolute (starts with "/") or relative (starts with directory name or "..")
4. ".." means go to parent directory
5. `pwd` should output the absolute path with no ".." and ending with "/"
6. We start at root directory "/"

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Track current directory path
- We'll maintain a list representing the current path from root to current directory
- Initially, this list is empty (but we'll represent root as empty list)

### Step 2: Process cd command
- If path starts with "/", it's absolute - we reset the current path and process the rest
- Otherwise, it's relative - we process from current path
- Split the path by "/" and process each component
- For each component:
  - If it's "..", remove the last directory from current path
  - If it's a directory name, add it to the current path
- Special case: if path is just "/" (root), we set current path to empty

### Step 3: Process pwd command
- Convert current path list to string with "/" separators
- Add trailing slash

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Handle edge cases like empty path (should be root "/")
- Ensure ".." doesn't go above root (guaranteed by problem)

Let me trace through the first example:
- Start: current_path = []
- pwd: print "/" (empty list becomes "/")
- cd /home/vasya: absolute path, current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd: print "/home/vasya/"
- cd ..: go to parent, current_path = ["home"]
- pwd: print "/home/"
- cd vasya/../petya: relative, process each part:
  - "vasya": add to path → ["home", "vasya"]
  - "..": remove last → ["home"]
  - "petya": add → ["home", "petya"]
- pwd: print "/home/petya/"

## Code Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names in current path

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("cd"):
        path_part = command[3:].strip()  # remove "cd " and strip whitespace
        
        if path_part == "/":
            # This means cd to root
            current_path = []
        elif path_part.startswith("/"):
            # Absolute path - start from root
            current_path = []
            path_components = path_part[1:].split("/")
            for component in path_components:
                if component == "..":
                    if current_path:  # only if path is not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif component:  # non-empty component
                    current_path.append(component)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            path_components = path_part.split("/")
            for component in path_components:
                if component == "..":
                    if current_path:  # only if path is not empty
                        current_path.pop()
                elif component:  # non-empty component
                    current_path.append(component)
    
    elif command == "pwd":
        # Convert current_path to string
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            path_string = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(path_string)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the edge cases and string handling. Let me reconsider the input format.

Looking at the second example:
- cd /a/b gives /a/b/
- pwd prints /a/b/
- cd ../a/b: this should go from /a/b to /a/a/b

Let me trace that:
- Start: current_path = []
- cd /a/b: absolute path, path_components = ["a", "b"]
  - current_path becomes ["a", "b"]
- pwd: prints /a/b/
- cd ../a/b: path_part = "../a/b"
  - path_components = ["..", "a", "b"]
  - ".." removes last element: current_path becomes ["a"]
  - "a" adds to path: becomes ["a", "a"]
  - "b" adds to path: becomes ["a", "a", "b"]
- pwd: prints /a/a/b/

But the expected output is /a/a/b/, which matches.

In my current implementation, when I split a path that starts with "/", I remove the leading "/" and split the rest. This seems correct.

Let me fix the code to handle the case where path_part is exactly "/":

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have an operating system shell with two commands: `cd` and `pwd`
2. Directories form a hierarchical tree structure with root "/"
3. `cd` command changes current directory based on a path
4. `pwd` command displays the current absolute path
5. Path can be absolute (starts with "/") or relative (starts with directory name)
6. ".." means go to parent directory
7. Output path must not contain ".." and must end with "/"

## Key Insights

1. We need to maintain the current directory path as a list of directory names
2. For absolute paths (start with "/"): reset the path and process the rest
3. For relative paths: process the path starting from current directory
4. When we encounter "..", we remove the last directory from our path
5. When we encounter a directory name, we add it to our path
6. The path should not contain ".." in the final output

## Step-by-step Solution

1. Initialize current path as empty list (representing root)
2. For each command:
   - If `pwd`: output the current path with "/" at beginning and end
   - If `cd`: parse the path and update current directory

Let me trace through the examples:

**Example 1:**
- Start: current_path = []
- pwd → "/" 
- cd /home/vasya → current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → "/home/vasya/"
- cd .. → current_path = ["home"] (removed "vasya")
- pwd → "/home/"
- cd vasya/../petya → vasya: add "vasya", then ..: remove last (now "home"), then petya: add "petya"
  → current_path = ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya/"

**Example 2:**
- cd /a/b → current_path = ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"
- cd ../a/b → ../: remove last ("b"), now ["a"], then add "a", then add "b" → ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"

Wait, let me recheck example 2:
- cd /a/b → current_path = ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"
- cd ../a/b → ../ means go to parent of current directory (which is "a"), so current_path becomes ["a"], then path is "../a/b" which means from current ["a"], go to parent (which is empty), then go to "a", then "b"
- Actually, we need to parse the entire path "../a/b"

Let me correct:
- cd ../a/b: the path is "../a/b"
- Since we're in directory ["a","b"], when we see "..", we go to parent: ["a"]
- Then we see "a", so we go to "a" (now ["a","a"])
- Then we see "b", so we go to "b" (now ["a","a","b"])

But looking at the expected output: "/a/a/b/" - that seems wrong.

Wait, let me re-read. The input is:
```
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd
```

The expected output is:
```
/a/b/
/a/a/b/
```

Ah I see - the second command is `cd ../a/b`, but this is a relative path from current directory.

When we are at `/a/b`, and do `cd ../a/b`:
- `../` means go to parent directory (which is `/a`)
- then `a/b` means go to directory named "a" in that location, then directory named "b"
- so from `/a`, go to `/a/a/b`

Wait, no - the path `../a/b` means:
1. Start from current directory `/a/b`
2. Go to parent with `..` → now at `/a`
3. Then go to directory "a" → now at `/a/a`
4. Then go to directory "b" → now at `/a/a/b`

So the path becomes `["a", "a", "b"]`

Yes, that matches the expected output `/a/a/b/`.

## Algorithm

1. Maintain current_path as a list of directory names (empty list means root)
2. For each command:
   - If it's "pwd": output current_path with formatting
   - If it's "cd path":
     - If path starts with "/", process absolute path
       - Remove existing path (clear current_path)
       - Process the rest of the path (after first "/")
     - Else (relative path):
       - Process the path from current directory
     - Parse the path by splitting on "/"
     - For each component:
       - If it's "..": remove last element from current_path (if exists)
       - If it's a directory name (non-empty string): append it to current_path

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("pwd"):
        # Format the current path
        path_str = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
        print(path_str)
    else:  # command starts with "cd"
        path_part = command.split(" ", 1)[1]  # Get the path part after "cd"
        
        if path_part.startswith("/"):
            # Absolute path
            current_path = []
            # Remove the leading "/"
            path_components = path_part[1:].split("/")
            for component in path_components:
                if component == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only remove if there are directories
                        current_path.pop()
                elif component:  # Non-empty component (skip empty strings)
                    current_path.append(component)
        else:
            # Relative path
            path_components = path_part.split("/")
            for component in path_components:
                if component == "..":
                    if current_path:  # Only remove if there are directories
                        current_path.pop()
                elif component:  # Non-empty component
                    current_path.append(component)
```

Let me test this with the examples.

Example 1:
- pwd → "/" (empty path)
- cd /home/vasya → path_part = "/home/vasya", starts with "/", so clear path, split to ["home", "vasya"]
  - "home": append → ["home"]
  - "vasya": append → ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → "/home/vasya/"
- cd .. → path_part = "..", starts with "/"? No, so relative path
  - split to [".."]
  - ".." → pop → path becomes ["home"]
- pwd → "/home/"
- cd vasya/../petya → path_part = "vasya/../petya", not starting with "/"
  - split to ["vasya", "..", "petya"]
  - "vasya" → append → ["home", "vasya"]
  - ".." → pop → ["home"]
  - "petya" → append → ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya/"

This matches expected output.

Example 2:
- cd /a/b → path_part = "/a/b", starts with "/":
  - clear path, split to ["a", "b"]
  - "a" → ["a"]
  - "b" → ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/"
- cd ../a/b → path_part = "../a/b", not starting with "/"
  - split to ["..", "a", "b"]
  - ".." → pop → [] (from ["a","b"] becomes [])
  - "a" → ["a"]
  - "b" → ["a", "b"]
  - Wait, this gives ["a","b"], but expected is "/a/a/b/"

I see the mistake. After `cd ../a/b` from current `["a","b"]`:
- Start with `["a","b"]`
- Process "..": remove last → becomes `["a"]`
- Then process "a": append → becomes `["a","a"]`
- Then process "b": append → becomes `["a","a","b"]`

In my code above, when I do `path_components = path_part.split("/")`, for "../a/b" it gives ["..", "a", "b"].

For relative path processing:
- Start with current_path = ["a", "b"]
- component = "..": remove last → current_path = ["a"]
- component = "a": append → current_path = ["a", "a"]
- component = "b": append → current_path = ["a", "a", "b"]

This is correct!

In my previous analysis I incorrectly assumed the current_path was reset. No, in relative path, we start with the current_path.

In my code, for the relative path case, we start with the existing current_path and modify it as we process each component.

But in the example, after cd /a/b, current_path is ["a","b"].

Then cd ../a/b:
- path_components = ["..", "a", "b"]
- component "..": since current_path is ["a","b"], remove last → becomes ["a"]
- component "a": append → ["a","a"]
- component "b": append → ["a","a","b"]

Yes, this gives ["a","a","b"] which formats to "/a/a/b/".

Perfect!

The code is correct.

```python

    if command.startswith("pwd"):
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
2. Directory structure is a tree with root "/"
3. Path can be absolute (starts with "/") or relative (starts without "/")
4. ".." means go to parent directory
5. We need to maintain the current directory path and output it properly
6. Output should not contain ".." and end with a slash

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Understand the directory navigation
- When we have an absolute path (starts with "/"), we start from root
- When we have a relative path (doesn't start with "/"), we start from current directory
- " ".." moves us to the parent directory
- Normal directory names move us to that subdirectory

### Step 2: Data structure
- Maintain a list (stack) of directory names to represent the current path
- Root starts as empty list (or just a single root)

### Step 3: Process each command
- For `pwd`: print the current path in format "/dir1/dir2/..."
- For `cd`: parse the path and update the current directory list

### Step 4: Parse path
- If path starts with "/", it's absolute - reset current path to empty
- Otherwise, it's relative - start from current path
- Split by "/" and process each component
- For "..": remove the last directory from current path
- For normal directory names: add to current path

### Step 5: Edge cases
- Path with only "/" means root
- Path with ".." at start (like "../something") - this should work
- Multiple ".." in path - each one removes one directory

Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1:
- Start: current_path = []
- pwd → "/" 
- cd /home/vasya → current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → "/home/vasya"
- cd .. → remove last → current_path = ["home"]
- pwd → "/home"
- cd vasya/../petya → 
  - relative: start from current_path = ["home"]
  - vasya → add → ["home", "vasya"]
  - .. → remove last → ["home"]
  - petya → add → ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya"

Wait, let me recheck the example: cd vasya/../petya
- Current is /home
- vasya/../petya: 
  - vasya: go to /home/vasya
  - ..: go to /home
  - petya: go to /home/petya
- So result should be "/home/petya/"

This matches.

## Implementation

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names, empty means root

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("pwd"):
        # Format the current path as "/dir1/dir2/..."
        result = "/" + "/".join(current_path) if current_path else "/"
        print(result)
    
    elif command.startswith("cd"):
        path_str = command.split(" ")[1]
        
        # Determine if it's absolute (starts with /) or relative
        if path_str.startswith("/"):
            # Absolute path - start from root
            current_path = []
            # Process the path after the leading /
            parts = path_str[1:].split("/")
            for part in parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # only pop if not at root
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # not empty (skip empty strings)
                    current_path.append(part)
        else:
            # Relative path - start from current directory
            parts = path_str.split("/")
            for part in parts:
                if part == "..":
                    if current_path:  # only pop if not at root
                        current_path.pop()
                elif part:  # not empty
                    current_path.append(part)
```

- path_str[1:] = "" → split("") gives []
- for each part: no parts → current_path remains empty → which is correct for root.


Problem

View Problem Statement 
You are an expert Python programmer. You will be given a question (problem specification) and will generate a correct Python program that matches the specification and passes all tests.

Vasya is writing an operating system shell, and it should have commands for working with directories. To begin with, he decided to go with just two commands: cd (change the current directory) and pwd (display the current directory).

Directories in Vasya's operating system form a traditional hierarchical tree structure. There is a single root directory, denoted by the slash character "/". Every other directory has a name — a non-empty string consisting of lowercase Latin letters. Each directory (except for the root) has a parent directory — the one that contains the given directory. It is denoted as "..".

The command cd takes a single parameter, which is a path in the file system. The command changes the current directory to the directory specified by the path. The path consists of the names of directories separated by slashes. The name of the directory can be "..", which means a step up to the parent directory. «..» can be used in any place of the path, maybe several times. If the path begins with a slash, it is considered to be an absolute path, that is, the directory changes to the specified one, starting from the root. If the parameter begins with a directory name (or ".."), it is considered to be a relative path, that is, the directory changes to the specified directory, starting from the current one.

The command pwd should display the absolute path to the current directory. This path must not contain "..".

Initially, the current directory is the root. All directories mentioned explicitly or passed indirectly within any command cd are considered to exist. It is guaranteed that there is no attempt of transition to the parent directory of the root directory.

Input

The first line of the input data contains the single integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 50) — the number of commands.

Then follow n lines, each contains one command. Each of these lines contains either command pwd, or command cd, followed by a space-separated non-empty parameter.

The command parameter cd only contains lower case Latin letters, slashes and dots, two slashes cannot go consecutively, dots occur only as the name of a parent pseudo-directory. The command parameter cd does not end with a slash, except when it is the only symbol that points to the root directory. The command parameter has a length from 1 to 200 characters, inclusive.

Directories in the file system can have the same names.

Output

For each command pwd you should print the full absolute path of the given directory, ending with a slash. It should start with a slash and contain the list of slash-separated directories in the order of being nested from the root to the current folder. It should contain no dots.

Examples

Input

7
pwd
cd /home/vasya
pwd
cd ..
pwd
cd vasya/../petya
pwd


Output

/
/home/vasya/
/home/
/home/petya/


Input

4
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd


Output

/a/b/

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have two commands: `cd` (change directory) and `pwd` (print working directory)
2. Directory structure is hierarchical (tree) with root at "/"
3. Path can be absolute (starts with "/") or relative (starts with directory name or "..")
4. ".." means go to parent directory
5. `pwd` should output the absolute path without ".." and ending with "/"
6. We start at root directory ("/")

## Step-by-step Solution

### Step 1: Track current path
- Maintain a list (or stack) that represents the current path from root
- Initially, current path is [""] (empty) or we can use [""] to represent root

### Step 2: Process cd command
- If path starts with "/", it's absolute - clear current path and parse the rest
- Otherwise, it's relative - keep current path and parse relative path
- For each part in the path:
  - If it's a directory name (lowercase letters), add it to current path
  - If it's "..", remove the last directory from current path (if not at root)

### Step 3: Process pwd command
- Convert current path list to string with "/" separator
- Add trailing "/" to the end

### Step 4: Implementation details
- Split the path by "/" and filter empty strings
- Handle the root case properly
- Be careful with the empty string after splitting

Let me trace through the first example:
- Start: current_path = []
- pwd → "/" 
- cd /home/vasya → absolute path, so current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → "/home/vasya/"
- cd .. → go to parent, so current_path = ["home"]
- pwd → "/home/"
- cd vasya/../petya → relative path from current directory
  - vasya → add "vasya" → current_path = ["home", "vasya"]
  - .. → remove last → current_path = ["home"]
  - petya → add "petya" → current_path = ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya/"

Wait, let me re-read the last example in input:
Input: 
cd /a/b
pwd
cd ../a/b
pwd

Expected output:
/a/b/
/a/a/b/

So after cd /a/b: path is /a/b
Then cd ../a/b: ../ means go to parent (b → a), then go to a/b
So from /a/b, go to parent: /a, then cd to a/b → /a/a/b

This means when we have a relative path like "../a/b", we:
1. Remove the last directory (go to parent)
2. Then go to the directory "a/b" from the current position

But in the second example, after cd /a/b, current path is ["a", "b"]
Then cd ../a/b: 
- ../ means remove last directory → ["a"]
- Then we have "a/b", which would be processed as: 
  - a → add "a" → ["a", "a"] 
  - b → add "b" → ["a", "a", "b"]

The expected output is /a/a/b/, which matches.

So the algorithm:
- For cd command:
  - If path starts with "/", split from index 1 (after leading slash)
  - Otherwise, keep current path as base
  - Parse each part in the path:
    - If part is "..", remove last element from current path
    - If part is a directory name (alphanumeric), append it
  - If absolute path, start with empty path and then process parts

Let me implement:

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names, empty initially (root)

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("cd"):
        path_str = command[3:].strip()  # remove "cd " and strip whitespace
        
        if path_str.startswith('/'):
            # absolute path - start fresh from root
            current_path = []
            path_parts = path_str[1:].split('/')  # remove leading slash
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == '':
                    continue
                if part == '..':
                    if current_path:  # can't go above root
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(part)
        else:
            # relative path - start from current directory
            path_parts = path_str.split('/')
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == '':
                    continue
                if part == '..':
                    if current_path:  # can't go above root
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(part)
                    
    elif command == "pwd":
        # Convert current_path list to absolute path
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            path_str = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(path_str)
```

Wait, let me check the edge case: when path is just "/", it should be handled.

In the input specification: "The command parameter cd does not end with a slash, except when it is the only symbol that points to the root directory."

So when path is "/", it's the root directory.

In my code, if path_str is "/", then path_str[1:] is empty, so path_parts is empty, so current_path remains empty.

Then when we do pwd, we print "/" which is correct.

Let me trace through the first example:
- n=7
- pwd → current_path empty → print "/"
- cd /home/vasya → path_str = "/home/vasya" → starts with / → current_path = []
  - path_parts = ["home", "vasya"]
  - home → append → ["home"]
  - vasya → append → ["home", "vasya"]
- pwd → join → "/home/vasya/" → correct
- cd .. → path_str = ".." → relative path? No, it's just ".."
  - path_parts = [".."]
  - part == ".." → pop → current_path becomes ["home"]
- pwd → "/home/" → correct
- cd vasya/../petya → path_str = "vasya/../petya" → no leading slash → relative
  - path_parts = ["vasya", "..", "petya"]
  - "vasya" → append → ["home", "vasya"]
  - ".." → pop → ["home"]
  - "petya" → append → ["home", "petya"]
- pwd → "/home/petya/" → correct

Second example:
- cd /a/b → path_str starts with / → current_path = []
  - path_parts = ["a", "b"]
  - append "a" → ["a"]
  - append "b" → ["a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/b/" → correct
- cd ../a/b → path_str = "../a/b" → no leading slash → relative
  - path_parts = ["..", "a", "b"]
  - ".." → pop → current_path becomes ["a"]
  - "a" → append → ["a", "a"]
  - "b" → append → ["a", "a", "b"]
- pwd → "/a/a/b/" → correct

Perfect!

One more edge case: what if we have path with consecutive ".."? The problem says "two slashes cannot go consecutively", so we don't need to worry.

Also, the problem says "It is guaranteed that there is no attempt of transition to the parent directory of the root directory."

So we don't need to check if current_path is empty before popping.

Final code:

```python
n = int(input())
current_path = []  # list of directory names, empty initially (root)

for _ in range(n):
    command = input().strip()
    
    if command.startswith("cd"):
        path_str = command[3:].strip()  # remove "cd " and strip whitespace
        
        if path_str.startswith('/'):
            # absolute path - start fresh from root
            current_path = []
            path_parts = path_str[1:].split('/')  # remove leading slash
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == '':
                    continue
                if part == '..':
                    if current_path:
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(part)
        else:
            # relative path - start from current directory
            path_parts = path_str.split('/')
            for part in path_parts:
                if part == '':
                    continue
                if part == '..':
                    if current_path:
                        current_path.pop()
                else:
                    current_path.append(part)
                    
    elif command == "pwd":
        # Convert current_path list to absolute path
        if not current_path:
            print("/")
        else:
            path_str = "/" + "/".join(current_path) + "/"
            print(path_str)
```
